# Unit — Class Overview

<!-- documentation: model-written from source via the local LLM; review before trusting -->

# Unit

`Unit` is the foundational C++ class representing any living, interactive entity in the VMaNGOS world—specifically `Player`, `Creature`, `Pet`, and `Totem`. It serves as the mechanical engine for combat, resource management, spell aura application, and movement, providing the shared logic that derived classes rely upon to simulate life, death, and interaction within the game world.

## How the class is split

The `Unit` implementation is distributed across three primary partials, each handling a distinct layer of the entity's behavior:

*   **`Unit.Main`**: The core identity and state machine. This partial manages the unit's vital statistics (health, power, stats), combat state (alive, dead, in-combat), threat and aggro lists, movement control (speeds, splines, flags), and relational data (owner, charmer, pet). It also handles low-level combat resolution, including melee swing outcomes, damage calculation, and immunity checks.
*   **`Unit.SpellAuras`**: The spell effect lifecycle manager. This partial defines the `Aura` and `SpellAuraHolder` structures and implements the logic for applying, stacking, refreshing, and removing spell effects. It contains the massive dispatch table for `AuraType` handlers (e.g., `HandleAuraModStat`, `HandlePeriodicDamage`) that dictate how specific spells modify the unit's state over time.
*   **`Unit.AuraProcHandler`**: The reactive trigger engine. This partial determines when active auras should "proc" (trigger secondary effects) in response to combat events like taking damage or casting spells. It evaluates conditions (chance rolls, equipment requirements, spell families) and executes the resulting effects, such as casting a new spell or dealing bonus damage.

## How the partials collaborate

The collaboration between these partials follows a clear directional flow driven by the server's update loop and event system:

1.  **State & Resources (`Unit.Main`)**: Acts as the source of truth for the unit's current condition. When `Unit.SpellAuras` needs to apply a stat modifier or heal damage, it queries `Unit.Main` for current values (e.g., `GetHealth`, `GetPower`) and invokes setters (e.g., `ModifyHealth`, `SetPower`). Conversely, `Unit.Main` delegates all complex spell-effect logic to `Unit.SpellAuras` when a spell hits or ticks.
2.  **Aura Lifecycle (`Unit.SpellAuras`)**: Manages the collection of active spells on the unit. During the periodic tick, it iterates through `SpellAuraHolder`s, executing their effects. If an effect involves a proc (e.g., a shield absorbing damage and triggering a counter-spell), it may invoke logic in `Unit.AuraProcHandler` or directly cast spells via `SpellCaster`.
3.  **Reactive Triggers (`Unit.AuraProcHandler`)**: Listens for combat events initiated by `Unit.Main` (such as `ProcDamageAndSpellFor`). It evaluates whether any active auras meet their trigger criteria. If they do, it executes the proc, often resulting in a new spell cast or damage event, which loops back into the `Unit.Main` combat resolution system.

External boundaries include interactions with `Spell` (for casting), `CreatureAI`/`PlayerAI` (for decision-making), and `World` (for global config rates). The class does not persist data directly; it relies on `Player` and `Creature` derived classes to handle database I/O.

## Data model

The `Unit` class itself does not directly query or mutate database tables. It operates entirely on in-memory state. However, its behavior is heavily influenced by static data loaded by `ObjectMgr` at startup:

*   **`spell_proc_event`**: Used by `Unit.AuraProcHandler` to define conditions under which auras trigger.
*   **`spell_template`**: Defines the base properties of spells that create auras, referenced by `Unit.SpellAuras` during application.
*   **`creature_template` / `player`**: Define base stats and powers, initialized into `Unit.Main` during construction or level-up.

## Where to go deeper

*   **`Unit.Main`**: Open this doc to understand health/power mechanics, combat state transitions, threat management, and movement control. Essential for debugging why a unit isn't attacking, moving, or dying correctly.
*   **`Unit.SpellAuras`**: Open this doc to investigate spell effect application, stacking rules, periodic ticks, and specific aura behaviors (e.g., why a buff isn't applying or a DOT isn't ticking).
*   **`Unit.AuraProcHandler`**: Open this doc to debug proc-based spells (e.g., shields, on-hit effects, PPM weapons). Explains how trigger conditions are evaluated and executed.
